<a id='question-123'></a>

### Question 123: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 23 (progress)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular basis and clinical manifestations of specific primary immunodeficiencies, particularly focusing on defects in lymphocyte development and function, specifically related to the *RAG1/RAG2* genes and the resulting severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) phenotype, including its impact on T-cell receptor (TCR) and B-cell receptor (BCR) diversity and function, and the associated risk of opportunistic infections and malignancies. (本题考察原发性免疫缺陷症的分子基础和临床表现，特别是 *RAG1/RAG2* 基因缺陷导致的重症联合免疫缺陷症 (SCID) 表型，包括其对 T 细胞受体 (TCR) 和 B 细胞受体 (BCR) 多样性和功能的影响，以及相关的机会性感染和恶性肿瘤风险。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant, born at term to non-consanguineous parents, presents with recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis media, sepsis) starting at 3 months of age, failure to thrive, and chronic diarrhea. He has not received any vaccinations. Physical examination reveals lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, and oral thrush. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows markedly reduced numbers of T cells (CD3+), B cells (CD19+), and NK cells (CD16+/CD56+). T-cell receptor (TCR) excision circle (TREC) analysis is negative, and V(D)J recombination assays demonstrate a complete absence of functional RAG1 and RAG2 proteins. Genetic testing confirms homozygous loss-of-function mutations in the *RAG1* gene. Which of the following immunological defects is most directly responsible for the patient's inability to mount an adaptive immune response?

(A) Defective class switching recombination due to mutations in *AID* (Activation-Induced Cytidine Deaminase).
(B) Impaired T-cell positive selection in the thymus due to mutations in *MHC class II genes*.
(C) Failure of V(D)J recombination in developing lymphocytes due to mutations in *RAG1/RAG2*.
(D) Defective immunoglobulin heavy chain transcription due to mutations in *μ heavy chain enhancer*.
(E) Impaired B-cell maturation due to mutations in *BTK* (Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Age and Presentation:** 6-month-old infant with recurrent severe infections (bacterial, fungal - thrush) starting early in life (3 months), failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea. This pattern strongly suggests a severe primary immunodeficiency affecting both humoral and cellular immunity.
- **Lack of Vaccinations:** The patient has not received vaccinations, which is common in infants with severe immunodeficiencies presenting early.
- **Physical Exam:** Lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly (can be seen in infections or malignancies), oral thrush (opportunistic fungal infection, indicating T-cell defect).
- **Flow Cytometry:** Markedly reduced numbers of T cells (CD3+), B cells (CD19+), and NK cells (CD16+/CD56+). This indicates a severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) phenotype, affecting multiple lymphocyte lineages.
- **TREC Analysis:** Negative TREC indicates a lack of newly generated T-cell receptor rearrangements, consistent with a defect in V(D)J recombination. TRECs are circular DNA fragments released during T-cell receptor rearrangement in the thymus.
- **V(D)J Recombination Assay:** Complete absence of functional RAG1 and RAG2 proteins. This is the definitive molecular diagnosis.
- **Genetic Testing:** Confirms homozygous loss-of-function mutations in the *RAG1* gene.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the immunological defect *most directly responsible* for the inability to mount an adaptive immune response, given the molecular defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **RAG1/RAG2 Function:** The Recombination Activating Genes 1 and 2 (*RAG1* and *RAG2*) encode enzymes essential for V(D)J recombination. V(D)J recombination is the process by which gene segments encoding the variable regions of T-cell receptors (TCRs) and B-cell receptors (BCRs, i.e., immunoglobulins) are rearranged to create a diverse repertoire of antigen receptors.
- **Mechanism of RAG Deficiency:** Loss-of-function mutations in *RAG1* or *RAG2* prevent V(D)J recombination from occurring in developing T and B lymphocytes in the bone marrow and thymus.
- **Consequences of RAG Deficiency:**
    - **T-cell Development:** T-cell precursors cannot rearrange their TCR genes. Consequently, functional T cells (CD4+ and CD8+) fail to develop and mature. This leads to severe cellular immunodeficiency.
    - **B-cell Development:** B-cell precursors cannot rearrange their immunoglobulin heavy and light chain genes. Consequently, functional B cells fail to develop and mature. This leads to severe humoral immunodeficiency.
    - **NK Cell Development:** While NK cells do not express TCRs or BCRs and do not undergo V(D)J recombination, severe RAG deficiency can sometimes affect NK cell development or function, although the primary defect is in T and B cells. The flow cytometry finding of reduced NK cells is consistent with the overall severe lymphoid aplasia seen in RAG deficiency.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** The lack of functional T and B cells results in SCID, characterized by severe susceptibility to opportunistic infections (bacterial, viral, fungal, protozoal), failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea, and increased risk of malignancy (especially lymphoma).
- **TREC and V(D)J Assays:** TREC analysis detects the byproducts of V(D)J recombination. A negative TREC indicates a lack of recent TCR rearrangement. V(D)J recombination assays directly assess the ability of lymphocytes to rearrange their receptor genes. Absence of functional RAG proteins directly blocks this process.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective class switching recombination due to mutations in *AID* (Activation-Induced Cytidine Deaminase).** *AID* is essential for class switch recombination (CSR) and somatic hypermutation (SHM) in B cells. Mutations in *AID* cause Hyper-IgM syndrome type 2, characterized by normal or elevated IgM levels but very low levels of IgG, IgA, and IgE. Patients have recurrent bacterial infections but typically have normal T-cell numbers and function. This does not fit the patient's presentation of severe T-cell lymphopenia and lack of functional TCR/BCR rearrangement.
- (B) **Impaired T-cell positive selection in the thymus due to mutations in *MHC class II genes*.** MHC class II molecules are crucial for positive selection of CD4+ T cells in the thymus. Defects in MHC class II genes (or associated proteins like CD74) cause MHC class II deficiency, a form of SCID. However, this primarily affects CD4+ T cells, while CD8+ T cells and B cells are usually present (though B cell function may be impaired due to lack of T cell help). The patient has severe lymphopenia affecting T, B, and NK cells, and the molecular defect is specifically in V(D)J recombination, not MHC expression.
- (D) **Defective immunoglobulin heavy chain transcription due to mutations in *μ heavy chain enhancer*.** Mutations in the *μ heavy chain enhancer* cause Hyper-IgM syndrome type 1, characterized by a block in immunoglobulin secretion. B cells are present, but they cannot switch classes or secrete immunoglobulins. T-cell numbers and function are typically normal. This does not fit the patient's presentation of severe T-cell lymphopenia and lack of functional TCR/BCR rearrangement.
- (E) **Impaired B-cell maturation due to mutations in *BTK* (Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase).** *BTK* is essential for B-cell development beyond the pre-B cell stage. Mutations in *BTK* cause X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA), characterized by a profound deficiency of B cells and immunoglobulins. T-cell numbers and function are normal. This does not fit the patient's presentation of severe T-cell lymphopenia and lack of functional TCR/BCR rearrangement.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | RAG1/RAG2 Deficiency (SCID) | Hyper-IgM Syndrome Type 1 (*μ enhancer*) | Hyper-IgM Syndrome Type 2 (*AID*) | MHC Class II Deficiency | XLA (*BTK*) |
|-------------------------|-----------------------------|------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------|--------------------------|-------------|
| **Primary Defect**      | V(D)J Recombination         | Ig Secretion                             | Class Switch Recombination        | T-cell Positive Selection| B-cell Maturation |
| **T Cells (CD3+)**      | Absent/Severely Reduced     | Normal                                    | Normal                            | Primarily CD4+ Reduced   | Normal      |
| **B Cells (CD19+)**     | Absent/Severely Reduced     | Present (but non-functional)             | Present (but non-functional)      | Present                  | Absent      |
| **NK Cells (CD16+/56+)** | Often Reduced               | Normal                                    | Normal                            | Normal                   | Normal      |
| **Ig Levels**           | All absent                  | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE                 | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE          | Normal/Low IgG/IgA/IgE   | All absent  |
| **TREC**                | Negative                    | Positive                                 | Positive                          | Positive                 | Positive    |
| **Gene Affected**       | *RAG1/RAG2*                 | *μ heavy chain enhancer*                 | *AID*                             | *MHC Class II genes*     | *BTK*       |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
RAG1/RAG2 deficiency is a cause of SCID characterized by a complete block in V(D)J recombination, leading to the absence of functional T and B lymphocytes. This is diagnosed by severe lymphopenia (T-, B-, NK-), negative TREC, and confirmed by genetic testing or lack of RAG protein function. Key differentials include other causes of SCID (e.g., ADA deficiency, IL2RG deficiency, MHC class II deficiency), Hyper-IgM syndromes, and XLA, which have distinct immunological profiles. (RAG1/RAG2 缺陷是 SCID 的一种类型，其特征是 V(D)J 重组完全阻断，导致功能性 T 和 B 淋巴细胞缺失。诊断依据包括严重的淋巴细胞减少 (T-、B-、NK-)、阴性 TREC 以及基因检测或 RAG 蛋白功能缺失的确认。主要鉴别诊断包括其他 SCID 类型 (例如 ADA 缺乏症、IL2RG 缺乏症、MHC II 类缺陷)、高 IgM 综合征和 XLA，它们具有不同的免疫学特征。)

<a id='question-124'></a>

### Question 124: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 24 (Maintaining)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular defect and resulting clinical manifestations in Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS), a primary immunodeficiency characterized by eczema, thrombocytopenia, and recurrent infections, due to mutations in the *WAS* gene affecting actin cytoskeleton regulation in hematopoietic cells. (本题考察Wiskott-Aldrich综合征 (WAS) 的分子缺陷及其临床表现，这是一种原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是湿疹、血小板减少症和复发性感染，这是由于 *WAS* 基因突变影响造血细胞中肌动蛋白细胞骨架的调控。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with severe eczema, petechiae, and recurrent otitis media. His mother reports frequent bleeding from minor cuts and nosebleeds. He has not received any live vaccines due to concerns about potential complications. Peripheral blood smear shows small platelets (microthrombocytopenia). Flow cytometry analysis of his peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals absent expression of the CD43 protein. Genetic testing confirms a mutation in the *WAS* gene located on the X chromosome. Which of the following cellular processes is most directly impaired in this patient's lymphocytes, leading to the observed immunodeficiency?

(A) T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling complex formation
(B) B-cell receptor (BCR) internalization and antigen presentation
(C) Actin cytoskeleton polymerization and cell motility
(D) Complement receptor-mediated phagocytosis by macrophages
(E) Natural killer (NK) cell cytotoxicity via perforin/granzyme pathway

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 3-year-old boy with eczema, petechiae (suggesting thrombocytopenia), recurrent otitis media (suggesting immunodeficiency).
- **Family History/Vaccination:** Not receiving live vaccines suggests a known or suspected immunodeficiency, particularly a T-cell defect.
- **Lab Findings:** Microthrombocytopenia (small platelets) is a classic feature. Absent CD43 expression on lymphocytes is a key diagnostic marker.
- **Genetic Testing:** Mutation in the *WAS* gene confirms the diagnosis of Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the direct cellular process impaired in lymphocytes due to the *WAS* gene mutation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS):** An X-linked recessive primary immunodeficiency caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene.
- ***WAS* Gene Product:** The *WAS* gene encodes the Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome protein (WASP).
- **WASP Function:** WASP is a crucial cytoplasmic protein involved in actin cytoskeleton regulation. It interacts with various signaling molecules and actin-binding proteins (like Arp2/3 complex, WAVE/Scar complex) to control actin polymerization, branching, and cell motility.
- **Impact on Lymphocytes:** In lymphocytes (T cells, B cells, NK cells), WASP is essential for proper immune synapse formation, cell migration, antigen presentation, and T-cell activation. Defective WASP leads to impaired actin polymerization, affecting processes like:
    - **Immunological Synapse Formation:** T cells cannot effectively form synapses with antigen-presenting cells (APCs).
    - **Cell Motility:** Lymphocytes have impaired migration to sites of infection or lymphoid organs.
    - **Cytokine Production:** Defective T-cell activation leads to reduced cytokine production.
    - **Splenic Marginal Zone B-cell Function:** WASP is important for the function of splenic marginal zone B cells, which are involved in responses to polysaccharide antigens.
- **Impact on Platelets:** WASP is also expressed in platelets and megakaryocytes. Its deficiency leads to defective platelet formation (microthrombocytopenia) and impaired platelet function.
- **Impact on Eczema:** The link between WASP deficiency and eczema is less clear but is a consistent clinical feature.
- **CD43 Expression:** CD43 (sialophorin) is a heavily glycosylated transmembrane protein expressed on leukocytes. Its expression is regulated by WASP. Loss of WASP function leads to reduced or absent CD43 expression on lymphocytes.
- **Clinical Triad:** The classic triad is eczema, microthrombocytopenia, and recurrent infections (especially encapsulated bacteria due to impaired polysaccharide responses and opportunistic infections due to T-cell defects).

##### 3. Linking WASP Deficiency to Actin Cytoskeleton (连接 WASP 缺陷与肌动蛋白细胞骨架)
- The *WAS* gene encodes WASP.
- WASP is a critical regulator of the actin cytoskeleton.
- Actin polymerization is essential for cell motility, cell shape changes, and the formation of structures like the immunological synapse.
- In lymphocytes, WASP deficiency impairs actin-dependent processes required for immune function.
- Therefore, actin cytoskeleton polymerization and cell motility are directly impaired.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling complex formation:** While T-cell activation is impaired in WAS, the primary defect is not in the initial formation of the TCR signaling complex (which involves CD3, zeta chain, etc.). The defect lies downstream in the signaling cascade involving actin rearrangement necessary for effective signaling and synapse formation. The TCR complex itself forms, but the downstream events mediated by WASP are defective.
- **(B) B-cell receptor (BCR) internalization and antigen presentation:** BCR internalization and antigen presentation are complex processes involving multiple proteins. While B-cell function is affected in WAS (especially responses to polysaccharide antigens), the fundamental defect in WASP is not specifically in BCR internalization. WASP is involved in actin dynamics required for various cellular processes, including those related to B-cell function, but the primary molecular target is the actin cytoskeleton.
- **(D) Complement receptor-mediated phagocytosis by macrophages:** WAS is primarily a lymphocyte and platelet disorder. Macrophages are generally less affected, and the defect in WASP does not directly impair complement receptor-mediated phagocytosis in macrophages. This process relies on different signaling pathways and cytoskeletal elements primarily in phagocytes.
- **(E) Natural killer (NK) cell cytotoxicity via perforin/granzyme pathway:** NK cell function can be impaired in WAS due to defective WASP expression, as WASP is involved in NK cell cytotoxicity. However, the fundamental defect is the disruption of actin dynamics, which underlies the impaired motility and cytotoxic granule release. Option (C) describes the underlying molecular mechanism more accurately than (E), which describes a specific functional outcome (cytotoxicity) that is affected *because* of the actin defect. The question asks for the *most direct* impairment.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS) | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) | Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID) | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | DiGeorge Syndrome |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | *WAS* gene (WASP protein)      | *BTK* gene (Bruton's tyrosine kinase) | Various genes (e.g., *IL2RG*, *RAG1/2*) | *CYBB* gene (NADPH oxidase subunits) | *TBX1* gene (Transcription factor) |
| **Cell Type Affected**  | Lymphocytes, Platelets         | B cells                           | T cells, B cells, NK cells             | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells           |
| **Key Clinical Features** | Eczema, Thrombocytopenia, Infections | Recurrent bacterial infections | Severe, early-onset infections        | Recurrent bacterial/fungal infections | Hypocalcemia, Cardiac defects, Thymic hypoplasia |
| **Molecular Mechanism** | Actin cytoskeleton dysregulation | Impaired B-cell development/signaling | Impaired lymphocyte development/function | Defective oxidative burst            | Thymic development defect |
| **CD Marker**           | CD43 absent/low                | CD19+, CD20+ (B cells normal #)    | Variable depending on type              | Normal                               | CD3+ (T cells low) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome is caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene, leading to a deficiency in the WASP protein. WASP is crucial for regulating the actin cytoskeleton in hematopoietic cells. This defect impairs lymphocyte function (especially T-cell synapse formation and motility) and platelet formation/function, resulting in the classic triad of eczema, microthrombocytopenia, and recurrent infections. Remember the absent CD43 expression as a key diagnostic marker. (Wiskott-Aldrich综合征由 *WAS* 基因突变引起，导致 WASP 蛋白缺乏。WASP 对于造血细胞中肌动蛋白细胞骨架的调控至关重要。这种缺陷会损害淋巴细胞功能（尤其是 T 细胞免疫突触形成和迁移能力）和血小板的形成/功能，从而导致湿疹、微血小板减少症和复发性感染的经典三联征。记住 CD43 表达缺失是关键的诊断标志。)

<a id='question-125'></a>

### Question 125: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 25 (mediated)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of primary immunodeficiencies affecting T-cell function, specifically focusing on the role of CD40 ligand (CD40L) in T-cell dependent B-cell activation, macrophage activation, and the resulting clinical manifestations, including susceptibility to specific infections and hyper-IgM syndrome. (考察T细胞功能缺陷的原发性免疫缺陷病，特别是CD40L在T细胞依赖性B细胞活化和巨噬细胞活化中的作用，以及由此产生的临床表现，包括对特定感染的易感性和高IgM综合征。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent sinopulmonary infections, chronic diarrhea, and failure to thrive. He has never received live vaccines due to concerns about potential complications. His mother reports that he has had multiple episodes of *Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia (PJP) and cryptosporidiosis, requiring hospitalization. He also has a history of severe varicella infection. Laboratory studies reveal normal levels of IgM but markedly decreased levels of IgG, IgA, and IgE. Flow cytometry analysis of his peripheral blood lymphocytes shows a normal number of CD4+ T cells but absent CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on their surface. His B-cell subset analysis shows normal numbers of B cells, but they fail to undergo class switching.

(A) Deficiency in Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK)
(B) Deficiency in the common gamma chain (γc)
(C) Deficiency in CD40 ligand (CD40L)
(D) Deficiency in adenosine deaminase (ADA)
(E) Deficiency in inducible costimulator (ICOS)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent sinopulmonary infections, chronic diarrhea, failure to thrive:** Suggests an underlying immunodeficiency, particularly affecting humoral immunity (sinopulmonary infections) and cell-mediated immunity (chronic diarrhea, opportunistic infections like cryptosporidiosis).
- **Never received live vaccines:** Indicates a known or suspected T-cell defect, as live vaccines can cause severe disseminated infections in immunocompromised individuals.
- ***Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia (PJP) and cryptosporidiosis:** These are classic opportunistic infections seen in T-cell deficiencies, particularly CD4+ T-cell defects. Cryptosporidiosis is especially characteristic of severe T-cell immunodeficiencies.
- **Severe varicella infection:** Varicella-zoster virus (VZV) infections can be severe and disseminated in patients with T-cell defects.
- **Normal IgM, decreased IgG, IgA, IgE:** This pattern is characteristic of hyper-IgM syndrome, where B cells can produce IgM but cannot class switch to other isotypes.
- **Normal CD4+ T cell count, absent CD40L expression:** This is the key diagnostic finding. CD40L is expressed on activated CD4+ T cells and is crucial for interacting with CD40 on B cells and macrophages. Absence of CD40L prevents T-cell dependent B-cell activation (leading to lack of class switching and antibody production) and macrophage activation (contributing to susceptibility to opportunistic infections).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **CD40L (CD154):** Expressed on activated CD4+ T cells. It binds to CD40 on B cells, dendritic cells, and macrophages.
    - **B-cell activation:** CD40L-CD40 interaction is essential for T-cell dependent B-cell activation, including germinal center formation, affinity maturation, and immunoglobulin class switching (from IgM to IgG, IgA, IgE). Without this interaction, B cells can produce IgM but cannot switch isotypes.
    - **Macrophage activation:** CD40L-CD40 interaction is also required for macrophage activation, which is crucial for killing intracellular pathogens like *Pneumocystis jirovecii* and *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*.
    - **Genetic basis:** The gene for CD40L is located on the X chromosome. Deficiency leads to X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome (HIGM1).
- **Clinical Manifestations of CD40L deficiency:**
    - **Hyper-IgM syndrome:** Normal or elevated IgM, low IgG, IgA, IgE.
    - **Susceptibility to opportunistic infections:** *Pneumocystis jirovecii*, *Cryptosporidium*, CMV, *Mycobacterium* species due to impaired macrophage activation and lack of specific antibody responses.
    - **Severe viral infections:** Varicella, enteroviruses.
    - **Neutropenia:** Can occur due to impaired macrophage function.
    - **Failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea.**
- **Differential Diagnosis:** Other primary immunodeficiencies must be considered.
    - **BTK deficiency (X-linked agammaglobulinemia):** Affects B-cell development, leading to absent B cells and panhypogammaglobulinemia (low IgM, IgG, IgA, IgE). Patients typically present with recurrent bacterial infections but not the severe opportunistic infections seen here.
    - **Common gamma chain deficiency (X-linked severe combined immunodeficiency, X-SCID):** Affects multiple cytokine receptor signaling pathways (IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, IL-21), leading to severe defects in T cells, NK cells, and B cells. Patients present early in life with severe opportunistic infections and failure to thrive. Flow cytometry shows absent T cells and NK cells.
    - **ADA deficiency (SCID):** Causes accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine) leading to lymphopenia and SCID phenotype.
    - **ICOS deficiency:** ICOS is another costimulatory molecule on T cells involved in B-cell help and T follicular helper cell development. Deficiency leads to hyper-IgM syndrome similar to CD40L deficiency, but often with less severe opportunistic infections.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Deficiency in Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK):** This causes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA). Patients have absent B cells and very low levels of all immunoglobulin isotypes (panhypogammaglobulinemia), including IgM. They primarily suffer from recurrent bacterial infections (e.g., encapsulated bacteria). The vignette describes normal IgM levels and severe opportunistic infections, inconsistent with BTK deficiency.
- (B) **Deficiency in the common gamma chain (γc):** This causes X-linked severe combined immunodeficiency (X-SCID). Patients have profound defects in T cells, NK cells, and B cells (due to lack of T-cell help). They present very early with severe opportunistic infections. Flow cytometry would show absent or very low numbers of T cells and NK cells, which contradicts the finding of normal CD4+ T cell numbers in the vignette.
- (D) **Deficiency in adenosine deaminase (ADA):** This causes a form of severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). It leads to the accumulation of toxic metabolites, causing lymphopenia affecting T cells, B cells, and NK cells. Patients present early with severe infections. The vignette describes normal CD4+ T cell numbers and a specific pattern of hyper-IgM, inconsistent with ADA deficiency.
- (E) **Deficiency in inducible costimulator (ICOS):** ICOS is another costimulatory molecule expressed on activated T cells that is important for T follicular helper cell function and B-cell help. Deficiency in ICOS also causes hyper-IgM syndrome (normal IgM, low IgG/IgA/IgE) due to impaired class switching. However, patients with ICOS deficiency typically have less severe susceptibility to opportunistic infections compared to CD40L deficiency. While it causes a similar immunoglobulin pattern, the severe PJP and cryptosporidiosis point more strongly towards CD40L deficiency, which also impairs macrophage activation. The vignette specifically mentions absent CD40L expression, making this the most direct fit.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | CD40L Deficiency (HIGM1) | BTK Deficiency (XLA) | γc Deficiency (X-SCID) | ADA Deficiency (SCID) | ICOS Deficiency (HIGM2) |
|-------------------------|--------------------------|-----------------------|------------------------|-----------------------|--------------------------|
| **Gene**                | CD40L (X-linked)         | BTK (X-linked)        | IL2RG (X-linked)       | ADA                  | ICOS                     |
| **Cell Type Affected**  | CD4+ T cells             | B cells               | T cells, NK cells, B cells | T cells, B cells, NK cells | T cells                  |
| **Immunoglobulins**     | High IgM, Low IgG/A/E    | Low all (panhypo)     | Low all                | Low all               | High IgM, Low IgG/A/E    |
| **B Cells**             | Normal number            | Absent                | Normal number          | Normal number         | Normal number            |
| **T Cells**             | Normal number, No CD40L   | Normal number         | Absent/Very Low        | Absent/Very Low       | Normal number            |
| **NK Cells**            | Normal number            | Normal number         | Absent/Very Low        | Absent/Very Low       | Normal number            |
| **Opportunistic Infections** | Yes (PJP, Crypto, CMV) | Less common           | Severe                 | Severe                | Less common than CD40L   |
| **Key Defect**          | T-B cell interaction, Macrophage activation | B cell development    | Cytokine signaling     | Purine metabolism      | T-B cell interaction    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The clinical presentation of recurrent opportunistic infections (PJP, Cryptosporidium), severe viral infections (varicella), normal IgM with low other isotypes, and absent CD40L expression on T cells is pathognomonic for X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome due to CD40L deficiency. This condition highlights the critical role of CD40L in T-cell dependent B-cell activation (class switching) and macrophage activation. Differentiating this from other primary immunodeficiencies like XLA (absent B cells, panhypogammaglobulinemia), X-SCID (absent T/NK cells), ADA-SCID (lymphopenia), and ICOS deficiency (similar Ig pattern but often milder infections) requires careful analysis of immunoglobulin levels, cell counts, and specific functional defects.

<a id='question-126'></a>

### Question 126: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 26 (immunity.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of primary immunodeficiencies, specifically focusing on the clinical manifestations and underlying immunological defects associated with defects in T-cell development and function, particularly DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome) and Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID), and how these defects predispose patients to specific infections and complications. (本题考察原发性免疫缺陷，特别是T细胞发育和功能缺陷，例如DiGeorge综合征和重症联合免疫缺陷（SCID），以及这些缺陷如何使患者易患特定感染和并发症。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-month-old infant, born full-term to healthy, non-consanguineous parents, presents with recurrent oral thrush (Candida albicans), failure to thrive, and persistent cough. Physical examination reveals a small, upturned nose, low-set ears, and a prominent forehead. The infant has had multiple episodes of pneumonia, initially diagnosed as community-acquired, but unresponsive to standard antibiotic therapy. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows markedly reduced numbers of CD3+ and CD4+ T cells, with normal numbers of CD19+ B cells and CD16+/CD56+ NK cells. Genetic testing reveals a heterozygous deletion on chromosome 22q11.2. The infant is subsequently scheduled for a hematopoietic stem cell transplant (HSCT) from a matched sibling donor. Post-transplant, the infant develops severe graft-versus-host disease (GVHD), characterized by skin rash, diarrhea, and liver dysfunction. Which of the following immunological defects is MOST likely responsible for the infant's predisposition to opportunistic infections and the subsequent severe GVHD following HSCT?

(A) Defective B-cell maturation leading to hypogammaglobulinemia and impaired antibody responses.
(B) Defective T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling pathway, impairing positive and negative selection in the thymus.
(C) Defective interleukin-2 (IL-2) receptor signaling, impairing T-cell proliferation and function.
(D) Defective thymic development and T-cell maturation due to abnormal migration of neural crest cells.
(E) Defective complement component C3, leading to impaired opsonization and increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Age and Presentation:** 3-month-old infant with recurrent oral thrush, failure to thrive, persistent cough, and recurrent pneumonia unresponsive to antibiotics. This points towards a severe immunodeficiency, likely affecting cell-mediated immunity, given the opportunistic infections (Candida, pneumonia).
*   **Physical Findings:** Small upturned nose, low-set ears, prominent forehead. These are characteristic facial features associated with DiGeorge syndrome.
*   **Immunological Findings:** Flow cytometry shows markedly reduced CD3+ and CD4+ T cells, normal B cells (CD19+) and NK cells (CD16+/CD56+). This pattern indicates a primary T-cell defect.
*   **Genetic Findings:** Heterozygous deletion on chromosome 22q11.2 confirms DiGeorge syndrome (also known as 22q11.2 deletion syndrome).
*   **Treatment and Complication:** HSCT from a matched sibling donor, followed by severe GVHD. GVHD occurs when donor T cells recognize the recipient's tissues as foreign and attack them. The severity of GVHD is often related to the degree of T-cell reconstitution or residual recipient T-cell activity. In this context, the underlying T-cell defect in DiGeorge syndrome is crucial.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **DiGeorge Syndrome (22q11.2 Deletion Syndrome):** This syndrome results from a microdeletion on chromosome 22q11.2, affecting the development of the third and fourth pharyngeal pouches. These pouches are critical for the development of the thymus, parathyroid glands, and parts of the heart.
*   **Thymic Hypoplasia/Aplasia:** The most significant immunological consequence is impaired thymic development (thymic hypoplasia or aplasia). The thymus is the site of T-cell maturation.
*   **T-Cell Deficiency:** Reduced thymic function leads to a deficiency in mature T lymphocytes (CD3+, CD4+, CD8+). The severity varies widely among affected individuals, ranging from mild T-cell lymphopenia to complete athymia (complete absence of the thymus) and severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID)-like phenotype.
*   **Clinical Manifestations:** The T-cell deficiency predisposes patients to opportunistic infections, particularly viral (e.g., Candida, Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia), fungal, and intracellular bacterial infections. The physical features (facial dysmorphism), hypocalcemia (due to parathyroid hypoplasia), and congenital heart defects are also characteristic.
*   **Flow Cytometry:** The finding of markedly reduced CD3+ and CD4+ T cells with normal B and NK cells is highly characteristic of DiGeorge syndrome.
*   **Graft-versus-Host Disease (GVHD):** HSCT is a curative treatment for severe DiGeorge syndrome. However, the residual T-cell function, even if limited, can contribute to GVHD. Donor T cells engraft and recognize host antigens as foreign. In DiGeorge syndrome, the host's T cells are deficient, but some residual T cells or other immune cells might still present antigens or react to donor cells, contributing to GVHD. The severity of GVHD can sometimes be paradoxically higher in patients with partial T-cell defects compared to those with complete SCID, possibly due to residual recipient immune reactivity. The underlying defect in T-cell development (thymic hypoplasia) is the root cause of the immunodeficiency and influences the post-transplant course, including GVHD risk.

##### 3. Linking the Defect to the Clinical Picture
The infant's presentation (recurrent opportunistic infections, failure to thrive), physical features, flow cytometry results (low T cells), and genetic confirmation (22q11.2 deletion) all point strongly to DiGeorge syndrome. The core immunological defect in DiGeorge syndrome is impaired thymic development due to abnormal migration of neural crest cells during embryogenesis, leading to deficient T-cell maturation. This deficiency explains the susceptibility to infections and the subsequent complications like severe GVHD post-HSCT.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective B-cell maturation leading to hypogammaglobulinemia and impaired antibody responses.** This describes conditions like X-linked agammaglobulinemia (Bruton's disease) or Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID). While these cause recurrent bacterial infections, they do not typically present with the characteristic facial features of DiGeorge syndrome, nor do they primarily involve T-cell deficiency (flow cytometry would show low B cells, not T cells). The infections in the vignette (Candida, pneumonia) are more typical of T-cell defects.
- (B) **Defective T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling pathway, impairing positive and negative selection in the thymus.** This describes conditions like Omenn syndrome or certain forms of SCID. While it affects T-cell development, it doesn't explain the thymic hypoplasia/aplasia or the specific constellation of features (facial dysmorphism, hypocalcemia) seen in DiGeorge syndrome. The genetic basis is different (e.g., mutations in RAG1/2, IL7R, JAK3).
- (C) **Defective interleukin-2 (IL-2) receptor signaling, impairing T-cell proliferation and function.** This describes X-linked SCID (due to mutations in the IL2RG gene, which encodes the common gamma chain shared by IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21 receptors). While it causes severe T-cell deficiency and susceptibility to opportunistic infections, it does not explain the thymic hypoplasia, facial features, or hypocalcemia characteristic of DiGeorge syndrome. Flow cytometry in X-linked SCID typically shows absent T cells and often absent NK cells, with normal or increased B cells.
- (E) **Defective complement component C3, leading to impaired opsonization and increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria.** This describes C3 deficiency, a component of the complement system. It primarily leads to susceptibility to pyogenic bacterial infections (especially Neisseria species). It does not cause T-cell deficiency, the characteristic features of DiGeorge syndrome, or the specific opportunistic infections seen in the vignette.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | DiGeorge Syndrome (22q11.2 Deletion) | X-linked SCID (IL2RG mutation) | XLA (BTK mutation) | CVID |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :---------------- | :--- |
| **Primary Defect**      | Thymic hypoplasia/aplasia            | Defective IL-2R signaling      | Defective B-cell maturation | Defective B-cell function/survival |
| **Affected Cells**      | T cells (primarily)                  | T cells, NK cells              | B cells           | B cells |
| **T Cell Count**        | Low to absent                       | Absent                        | Normal            | Normal |
| **B Cell Count**        | Normal                              | Normal/Increased               | Absent            | Low/Normal |
| **NK Cell Count**       | Normal                              | Absent                        | Normal            | Normal |
| **Immunoglobulins**     | Normal (usually)                     | Normal (usually)               | Very low          | Low |
| **Key Infections**      | Opportunistic (Candida, Pneumocystis) | Opportunistic (Candida, Pneumocystis, CMV) | Encapsulated bacteria | Encapsulated bacteria |
| **Other Features**      | Facial dysmorphism, hypocalcemia, heart defects | None specific                 | None specific     | None specific |
| **Genetic Basis**       | 22q11.2 deletion                    | IL2RG mutation                | BTK mutation      | Variable |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion) is characterized by thymic hypoplasia/aplasia leading to T-cell deficiency, often presenting with opportunistic infections, facial dysmorphism, hypocalcemia, and congenital heart defects. The underlying defect is abnormal neural crest cell migration affecting pharyngeal pouch development. Differentiating it from other SCID forms (like X-linked SCID due to IL2RG defects or RAG defects) relies on recognizing the specific constellation of clinical features and flow cytometry patterns (normal B/NK cells in DiGeorge vs. absent T/NK cells in X-linked SCID). Remember that DiGeorge syndrome is caused by a structural deletion, not a single gene mutation affecting T-cell signaling pathways directly.

<a id='question-127'></a>

### Question 127: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 27 (linked)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the underlying immunological defect in Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS), specifically the role of the WAS protein (WASP) in actin cytoskeleton regulation and its impact on T-cell function, platelet size, and eczema. (本题考察Wiskott-Aldrich综合征（WAS）的潜在免疫缺陷，特别是WASP蛋白在actin细胞骨架调控中的作用，以及它对T细胞功能、血小板大小和湿疹的影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent bacterial infections (otitis media, pneumonia), eczema, and thrombocytopenia (small platelets). His mother reports a history of bleeding gums and nosebleeds. Family history reveals that his maternal uncle died at age 10 from lymphoma. Immunological workup shows decreased T-cell numbers, particularly CD8+ T cells, and impaired T-cell proliferation in response to mitogens. Flow cytometry reveals absent expression of the WAS protein in his lymphocytes. Which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for this patient's clinical presentation?

(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells, leading to impaired B-cell class switching and T-cell-dependent antibody responses.
(B) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) activity, resulting in a block in B-cell development and agammaglobulinemia.
(C) Defective WAS protein (WASP) function, impairing actin cytoskeleton reorganization and signal transduction in hematopoietic cells.
(D) Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3) activity, leading to impaired signaling downstream of cytokine receptors, particularly the common gamma chain (γc).
(E) Defective complement component C3, resulting in increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria due to impaired opsonization and membrane attack complex formation.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 3-year-old boy. This age is typical for the onset of symptoms in primary immunodeficiencies.
- **Clinical Triad:** Recurrent bacterial infections (otitis media, pneumonia), eczema, and thrombocytopenia (small platelets). This triad is highly characteristic of Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS).
- **Bleeding History:** Bleeding gums and nosebleeds are consistent with thrombocytopenia.
- **Family History:** Maternal uncle died young from lymphoma. WAS patients have an increased risk of autoimmunity and malignancy, including lymphoma. The maternal inheritance pattern is also consistent with an X-linked recessive disorder like WAS.
- **Immunological Findings:** Decreased T-cell numbers (especially CD8+), impaired T-cell proliferation. This points to a T-cell defect.
- **Diagnostic Test:** Absent WAS protein expression on flow cytometry. This is the definitive diagnostic finding for WAS.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular defect responsible for the clinical presentation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS):** An X-linked recessive primary immunodeficiency caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene, which encodes the Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome protein (WASP).
- **WASP Function:** WASP is a cytoplasmic protein crucial for actin cytoskeleton organization and signal transduction in hematopoietic cells, particularly lymphocytes and platelets. It interacts with various signaling molecules and actin-binding proteins (like Arp2/3 complex, WAVE/Scar complex) to regulate actin polymerization, cell motility, adhesion, and synapse formation.
- **Pathophysiology:**
    - **T-cells:** WASP is essential for T-cell receptor (TCR) signaling, T-cell activation, proliferation, and migration. Defective WASP leads to impaired T-cell function, reduced numbers (especially CD8+), and defective responses to mitogens.
    - **B-cells:** While B-cell numbers are usually normal, antibody responses (especially to polysaccharide antigens) can be impaired due to defective T-cell help.
    - **Platelets:** WASP is required for normal platelet formation and function. Defective WASP results in thrombocytopenia and characteristically *small* platelets (microthrombocytopenia) due to impaired megakaryocyte proplatelet formation.
    - **Eczema:** The mechanism is not fully understood but may involve dysregulated immune responses or impaired skin barrier function.
    - **Autoimmunity/Malignancy:** Increased risk is linked to defective immune regulation and potentially impaired lymphocyte apoptosis.
- **Molecular Defect:** The core defect is the absence or dysfunction of the WASP protein due to mutations in the *WAS* gene. This disrupts actin cytoskeleton dynamics and downstream signaling pathways critical for immune cell function and platelet development.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression:** This causes X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal or elevated IgM levels but low IgG, IgA, and IgE due to impaired T-cell help for B-cell class switching. They present with recurrent bacterial infections, especially opportunistic infections like *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not typical features.
- (B) **Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) activity:** This causes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA). BTK is crucial for B-cell development. Patients lack mature B cells and have very low levels of all immunoglobulin isotypes, leading to recurrent bacterial infections, particularly with encapsulated organisms. T-cell function is normal, and eczema/thrombocytopenia are not features.
- (D) **Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3) activity:** This causes X-linked severe combined immunodeficiency (X-SCID). JAK3 is essential for signaling downstream of several cytokine receptors, including the common gamma chain (γc), which is shared by receptors for IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. Patients have profound defects in T-cell and NK-cell development and function, leading to severe, early-onset infections. B-cell numbers may be normal, but function is impaired due to lack of T-cell help. Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not typical features.
- (E) **Defective complement component C3:** This causes a severe deficiency in the alternative and classical/lectin complement pathways. Patients are highly susceptible to recurrent infections with encapsulated bacteria (e.g., *Streptococcus pneumoniae*, *Haemophilus influenzae*, *Neisseria meningitidis*) due to impaired opsonization and bacterial lysis. It does not typically cause eczema, thrombocytopenia, or T-cell defects.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS) | X-linked Hyper-IgM Syndrome (CD40L defect) | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA, BTK defect) | X-linked SCID (JAK3 defect) | C3 Deficiency |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------|
| Inheritance         | X-linked Recessive              | X-linked Recessive                          | X-linked Recessive                           | X-linked Recessive          | Autosomal    |
| Key Defect          | WASP protein                    | CD40L expression                            | BTK activity                                 | JAK3 activity               | C3 protein   |
| Cell Type Affected  | T-cells, Platelets               | T-cells (help)                               | B-cells                                      | T-cells, NK cells           | N/A          |
| Immunoglobulins     | Variable, often low IgM          | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE                    | Very low all Ig classes                       | Normal B cells, low Ig      | Normal       |
| T-cell Function     | Impaired                        | Impaired (help)                              | Normal                                       | Severely impaired           | Normal       |
| B-cell Function     | Impaired (polysaccharide)        | Impaired (class switch)                      | Absent                                       | Impaired (lack T-cell help)  | Normal       |
| Platelets           | Thrombocytopenia, small size     | Normal                                       | Normal                                       | Normal                      | Normal       |
| Eczema              | Common                          | Uncommon                                     | Uncommon                                     | Uncommon                    | Uncommon     |
| Infections          | Bacterial, Opportunistic         | Opportunistic (e.g., *Pneumocystis*)         | Encapsulated bacteria                        | Severe, broad spectrum      | Encapsulated bacteria |
| Malignancy Risk     | Increased (lymphoma)             | Increased (lymphoma)                         | Increased (lymphoma)                          | Increased (lymphoma)        | N/A          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome is characterized by the triad of eczema, thrombocytopenia (with small platelets), and recurrent infections, along with an increased risk of autoimmunity and malignancy. The underlying defect is in the WASP protein, crucial for actin cytoskeleton regulation in hematopoietic cells. Differentiate WAS from other X-linked immunodeficiencies like XLA (B-cell defect, agammaglobulinemia), X-SCID (severe T-cell defect), and Hyper-IgM syndrome (T-cell help defect for B-cell class switching). Remember the characteristic small platelets in WAS.

<a id='question-128'></a>

### Question 128: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 28 (syndrome)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 28 (syndrome), specifically the underlying defect in DNA repair leading to immunodeficiency, autoimmunity, and malignancy, and its characteristic features. (本题考察原发性免疫缺陷病：Part 3 Focus 28 (syndrome) 的理解，特别是 DNA 修复缺陷导致的免疫缺陷、自身免疫和恶性肿瘤，以及其特征表现。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents with recurrent sinopulmonary infections, including pneumonia and otitis media, starting in infancy. He also has a history of severe eczema and multiple episodes of oral thrush. Physical examination reveals a small, round, erythematous rash on his face. His mother reports that he has been diagnosed with type 1 diabetes mellitus at age 4. His older brother died at age 10 from an aggressive lymphoma. Laboratory studies show markedly elevated levels of alpha-fetoprotein (AFP). Genetic testing reveals a mutation in the *ATM* gene.

(A) Deficiency in DNA double-strand break repair leading to chromosomal instability and increased risk of malignancy.
(B) Defective T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the *ZAP70* gene.
(C) Impaired B-cell class switching and somatic hypermutation due to mutations in the *AID* gene.
(D) Defective phagocyte NADPH oxidase activity leading to recurrent bacterial and fungal infections.
(E) Impaired T-cell and B-cell development due to mutations in the *RAG1/RAG2* genes.

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent sinopulmonary infections, eczema, oral thrush:** Suggests an underlying immunodeficiency, potentially involving both humoral and cellular immunity, as well as skin barrier dysfunction.
- **Type 1 diabetes mellitus:** Points towards autoimmunity, a common feature in certain primary immunodeficiencies.
- **Aggressive lymphoma in older brother:** Indicates a predisposition to malignancy, particularly lymphoid malignancies.
- **Elevated alpha-fetoprotein (AFP):** A characteristic laboratory finding in Ataxia-Telangiectasia (A-T).
- **Mutation in the *ATM* gene:** Confirms the diagnosis of Ataxia-Telangiectasia.
- **Ataxia-Telangiectasia (A-T):** This syndrome is characterized by progressive cerebellar ataxia (though not explicitly mentioned, it's the hallmark), oculocutaneous telangiectasias (the facial rash), immunodeficiency (recurrent infections), autoimmunity (type 1 diabetes), and increased risk of malignancy (lymphoma). The underlying defect is in the *ATM* gene, which encodes a protein kinase crucial for DNA damage response, particularly DNA double-strand break repair. This defect leads to genomic instability, chromosomal aberrations, and increased susceptibility to ionizing radiation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **ATM Protein Function:** The ATM (Ataxia-Telangiectasia Mutated) protein is a serine/threonine kinase activated by DNA double-strand breaks (DSBs). Upon activation, it phosphorylates numerous downstream targets, including p53, CHK2, and BRCA1, initiating cell cycle arrest, DNA repair, or apoptosis.
- **Defect in A-T:** Mutations in *ATM* impair the cell's ability to sense and repair DSBs. This leads to:
    - **Genomic Instability:** Accumulation of chromosomal breaks and rearrangements, contributing to cancer predisposition (especially lymphoid malignancies) and neurological degeneration (ataxia).
    - **Immunodeficiency:** Impaired V(D)J recombination in lymphocytes, leading to reduced diversity of T-cell receptors (TCRs) and B-cell receptors (BCRs), resulting in defective cellular and humoral immunity. This explains the recurrent infections.
    - **Autoimmunity:** Dysregulation of lymphocyte development and function, potentially leading to autoimmunity (e.g., type 1 diabetes).
    - **Elevated AFP:** The mechanism is not fully understood but is a consistent finding in A-T patients.
- **Relevance to Options:**
    - Option A directly describes the core defect in A-T: impaired DNA double-strand break repair, leading to chromosomal instability and malignancy risk.
    - Option B describes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA) due to *BTK* mutations, characterized by defective B-cell development and lack of antibodies.
    - Option C describes Hyper-IgM syndrome type 2 due to *AID* mutations, characterized by impaired class switching and somatic hypermutation.
    - Option D describes Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) due to NADPH oxidase defects, characterized by defective phagocyte killing of catalase-positive organisms.
    - Option E describes Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID) due to *RAG1/RAG2* mutations, characterized by a complete lack of functional T and B cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Deficiency in DNA double-strand break repair leading to chromosomal instability and increased risk of malignancy.** This is the correct description of the underlying defect in Ataxia-Telangiectasia (A-T), caused by mutations in the *ATM* gene. The clinical presentation (recurrent infections, autoimmunity, malignancy history, elevated AFP) and the confirmed *ATM* mutation strongly support this mechanism.
- (B): **Defective T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the *ZAP70* gene.** This describes ZAP-70 deficiency, a form of SCID characterized by impaired T-cell and NK-cell signaling, leading to severe immunodeficiency. Patients typically present with severe, early-onset infections. This does not fit the constellation of symptoms (ataxia, telangiectasias, autoimmunity, elevated AFP, malignancy predisposition) or the *ATM* mutation.
- (C): **Impaired B-cell class switching and somatic hypermutation due to mutations in the *AID* gene.** This describes Hyper-IgM syndrome type 2. Patients have normal or elevated IgM levels but low levels of IgG, IgA, and IgE. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections. This does not explain the ataxia, telangiectasias, autoimmunity, malignancy predisposition, or elevated AFP seen in the vignette.
- (D): **Defective phagocyte NADPH oxidase activity leading to recurrent bacterial and fungal infections.** This describes Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). Patients have recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms (e.g., *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus*). This does not explain the ataxia, telangiectasias, autoimmunity, malignancy predisposition, elevated AFP, or the *ATM* mutation.
- (E): **Impaired T-cell and B-cell development due to mutations in the *RAG1/RAG2* genes.** This describes a form of Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID). Patients have a profound deficiency of both T and B cells, leading to severe, early-onset infections. While immunodeficiency is present in the vignette, the specific features (ataxia, telangiectasias, autoimmunity, malignancy predisposition, elevated AFP) and the *ATM* mutation point away from RAG deficiency.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Ataxia-Telangiectasia (A-T) (*ATM* mutation) | XLA (*BTK* mutation) | Hyper-IgM2 (*AID* mutation) | CGD (NADPH Oxidase Defect) | SCID (*RAG1/RAG2* mutation) |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :------------------- | :------------------------- | :------------------------- | :-------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | DNA DSB Repair                             | B-cell Development   | Class Switching/SHM       | Phagocyte Killing          | T/B-cell Development        |
| **Key Symptoms**        | Ataxia, Telangiectasias, Immunodeficiency, Autoimmunity, Malignancy | Recurrent Infections | Recurrent Infections       | Recurrent Infections       | Severe Early Infections    |
| **Immune Cell Affected** | Lymphocytes (V(D)J recombination), All cells | B-cells              | B-cells                    | Phagocytes                 | T & B cells                 |
| **Lab Findings**        | Elevated AFP, Chromosomal Instability        | Low Immunoglobulins | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE   | Abnormal DHR Test          | Absent T/B cells            |
| **Gene Involved**       | *ATM*                                       | *BTK*                | *AID*                      | *CYBB* (most common)       | *RAG1/RAG2*                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Ataxia-Telangiectasia (A-T) is a primary immunodeficiency caused by mutations in the *ATM* gene, leading to defective DNA double-strand break repair. This results in a characteristic triad of progressive cerebellar ataxia, oculocutaneous telangiectasias, and immunodeficiency (often involving both T and B cells). Patients are also prone to autoimmunity, increased risk of malignancy (especially lymphoma), and often have elevated alpha-fetoprotein (AFP) levels. Key differentials for immunodeficiency include XLA (B-cell defect), CGD (phagocyte defect), Hyper-IgM syndromes (B-cell class switching defect), and SCID (severe T/B-cell defect). The combination of neurological signs (ataxia), skin findings (telangiectasias), immunodeficiency, autoimmunity, malignancy predisposition, and elevated AFP is highly specific for A-T.

<a id='question-129'></a>

### Question 129: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 3 Focus 29 (Deficiency)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of primary immunodeficiencies, specifically focusing on the molecular defect in Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS) and its clinical manifestations, including eczema, thrombocytopenia, recurrent infections, and increased risk of autoimmunity and malignancy. It requires differentiating WAS from other primary immunodeficiencies based on specific clinical features and immunological findings. (本题考察原发性免疫缺陷病，特别是Wiskott-Aldrich综合征(WAS)的分子缺陷及其临床表现，包括湿疹、血小板减少症、复发性感染以及自身免疫和恶性肿瘤的风险增加。需要根据具体的临床特征和免疫学发现区分WAS与其他原发性免疫缺陷病。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with a history of severe, persistent eczema since infancy, frequent bleeding episodes (petechiae, purpura, epistaxis), and recurrent sinopulmonary infections (otitis media, pneumonia). His mother reports that he has had difficulty gaining weight and has a small head circumference. Physical examination reveals widespread eczematous lesions, petechiae on the lower extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Laboratory studies show thrombocytopenia (platelet count 25,000/µL) with small platelets, normal lymphocyte counts, and decreased serum IgM levels. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals absent expression of CD43 (BCR signaling molecule) on T cells and B cells. The patient is subsequently diagnosed with Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome. Which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for the clinical and immunological findings in this patient?

(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells, leading to impaired B cell class switching and T cell-dependent B cell activation.
(B) Defective WASp protein expression, impairing actin cytoskeleton reorganization and signal transduction in hematopoietic cells.
(C) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) expression, leading to a block in B cell development at the pre-B cell stage.
(D) Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3) expression, resulting in impaired signaling downstream of cytokine receptors, particularly the common gamma chain (γc).
(E) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA) expression, leading to the accumulation of toxic deoxyadenosine metabolites in lymphocytes.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 3-year-old boy with eczema, thrombocytopenia (small platelets), recurrent infections, failure to thrive, and small head circumference. This triad of eczema, thrombocytopenia, and recurrent infections is highly suggestive of Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Thrombocytopenia with small platelets is a characteristic feature of WAS. Decreased serum IgM levels indicate impaired humoral immunity.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Absent expression of CD43 (also known as leukosialin or Sialophorin) on T cells and B cells is a key diagnostic finding in WAS. CD43 is involved in cell adhesion and signaling, and its absence is linked to the underlying molecular defect.
- **Diagnosis:** The patient is diagnosed with Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular defect responsible for the clinical and immunological findings.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS):** WAS is an X-linked recessive primary immunodeficiency caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene located on the X chromosome (Xp11.23).
- **WASp Protein:** The *WAS* gene encodes the Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome protein (WASp). WASp is a cytoplasmic protein involved in actin cytoskeleton organization, cell motility, adhesion, and signal transduction in hematopoietic cells, particularly lymphocytes and platelets.
- **Molecular Defect:** Mutations in the *WAS* gene lead to absent or dysfunctional WASp protein.
- **Pathophysiology:** Lack of functional WASp disrupts actin polymerization and signaling pathways crucial for immune cell function (e.g., T cell receptor signaling, B cell receptor signaling, phagocytosis, chemotaxis) and platelet function (e.g., platelet adhesion, aggregation, granule release).
- **Clinical Manifestations:** The classic triad includes eczema, thrombocytopenia (with small platelets), and recurrent infections (due to impaired T cell and B cell function). Patients are also prone to autoimmunity and malignancies (lymphoma, leukemia).
- **Immunological Findings:** Variable immunodeficiency, often involving impaired T cell function (especially cytotoxic T cells and T helper cells), defective antibody responses (low IgM, normal or high IgA/IgE), and impaired phagocytosis. Flow cytometry often shows absent or reduced CD43 expression on lymphocytes.
- **CD43:** CD43 is a heavily glycosylated transmembrane protein expressed on most leukocytes. It plays roles in cell adhesion, migration, and signaling. Its expression is regulated by WASp.

Now let's analyze the options:
- **(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L):** This causes X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal or elevated IgM but low IgG, IgA, and IgE due to impaired T cell-dependent B cell class switching and activation. They present with recurrent bacterial infections, particularly opportunistic infections like *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not typical features.
- **(B) Defective WASp protein expression:** This is the underlying molecular defect in Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome. It explains the eczema, thrombocytopenia (due to platelet dysfunction), recurrent infections (due to impaired lymphocyte function), and the characteristic flow cytometry finding of absent CD43 expression. This matches the clinical vignette perfectly.
- **(C) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK):** This causes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA). Patients have a profound deficiency of B cells and all immunoglobulin isotypes due to a block in B cell development. They present with recurrent bacterial infections, especially encapsulated organisms, starting after maternal antibodies wane. Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not features.
- **(D) Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3):** This causes X-linked severe combined immunodeficiency (X-SCID). JAK3 is essential for signaling downstream of several cytokine receptors, including the common gamma chain (γc). Patients have profound defects in T cell and NK cell development, and often B cell development as well. They present early in life with severe, life-threatening infections (bacterial, viral, fungal, opportunistic). Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not typical features.
- **(E) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA):** This causes autosomal recessive severe combined immunodeficiency (ADA-SCID). ADA deficiency leads to the accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine triphosphate) in lymphocytes, causing lymphopenia and severe immunodeficiency affecting T, B, and NK cells. Patients present early with severe infections. Eczema and thrombocytopenia are not typical features.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L):** Incorrect. This causes Hyper-IgM syndrome, characterized by normal IgM but low IgG/IgA/IgE, and lacks the characteristic eczema and thrombocytopenia of WAS. The vignette describes low IgM, not high IgM.
- **(B) Defective WASp protein expression:** Correct. This is the specific molecular defect in Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome, explaining the triad of eczema, thrombocytopenia (small platelets), recurrent infections, and the absent CD43 expression.
- **(C) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK):** Incorrect. This causes XLA, characterized by a lack of B cells and panhypogammaglobulinemia. Patients lack eczema and thrombocytopenia.
- **(D) Defective Janus kinase 3 (JAK3):** Incorrect. This causes X-SCID, a severe combined immunodeficiency affecting T and NK cells primarily. Patients lack eczema and thrombocytopenia.
- **(E) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA):** Incorrect. This causes ADA-SCID, another form of SCID affecting T, B, and NK cells. Patients lack eczema and thrombocytopenia.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Wiskott-Aldrich Syndrome (WAS) | X-linked Hyper-IgM Syndrome | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) | X-linked SCID (JAK3 Deficient) | ADA-SCID |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|------------------------------|-----------------------------------|---------------------------------|----------|
| **Gene Defect**      | *WAS* (WASp)                    | *CD40LG* (CD40L)             | *BTK*                             | *JAK3*                          | *ADA*    |
| **Inheritance**      | X-linked Recessive              | X-linked Recessive           | X-linked Recessive                | X-linked Recessive              | AR      |
| **Key Features**     | Eczema, Thrombocytopenia (small platelets), Recurrent Infections | Recurrent Infections, High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE | Recurrent Infections, No B cells, Low Ig | Severe Infections (early), No T/NK cells | Severe Infections (early), Lymphopenia |
| **Immunoglobulins**  | Low IgM, Normal/High IgA/IgE   | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE    | Very Low All Ig                  | Normal/Low All Ig               | Low All Ig |
| **Lymphocytes**      | Variable T/B cell dysfunction   | Normal T/B cell numbers      | Absent B cells                    | Absent T/NK cells               | Low T/B/NK |
| **CD43 Expression**  | Absent/Reduced                  | Normal                       | Normal                            | Normal                          | Normal   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome is characterized by the triad of eczema, thrombocytopenia (with small platelets), and recurrent infections, caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene leading to defective WASp protein. Remember to differentiate WAS from other primary immunodeficiencies like X-linked Hyper-IgM (CD40L defect, high IgM), XLA (BTK defect, absent B cells), and SCID (JAK3 or ADA defect, severe T cell deficiency) based on their distinct clinical and immunological features. The absent CD43 expression is a key diagnostic clue for WAS.
